The presentifying experiences are in primally original unity in the stream and as such polarized in the primally original ego. But they stand in a primally original coincidence, a unity of identity as to the ego presentified in them with the primally originally functioning ego of the living present. Every experience, even anonymous, has an ego-pole; reflection discloses it as identical with the reflecting ego. Presentifications carry a presentified ego coinciding with the present one. This "coincidence of ego-poles" is the criterion of ownness the appendix develops.

(350–351) Everything conscious for me as an object (object-pole) in the stream of the living present, in whatever modes, is related to the primally original ego (the constantly perceptual one). Everything temporalized, conscious through presentifications, temporalization, has the ego as one and the same in itself, every presentifying intuition through originally unifying identification with the primally originally conscious ego. And that goes further into the intentional mediacies. Also through all possible experiences (I could have experienced so, I could have perceived so), identity of the ego now becoming primally originally conscious for me, also through all imaginative presentifications; the coincidence reaches in there too. This is a structure running through the whole of conscious life, the universal unity of coincidence of all lived experiences as regards the ego functioning originaliter or presentifiedly in them (whether it has disclosed the ego reflectively or not, but which in any case carries it implicitly, anonymously) with the "standing," primally originally functioning ego, through a constant coincidence actually unifying the actually presentified or unreflectedly co-presentified experiences. The empty anticipations, their mediacies, the horizons: in them lies anticipated constantly also anticipation of the coincidences disclosed in disclosure. Universal coincidence of ego-poles runs through memory, possibility, even phantasy: the "standing" ego is identical in all. This is the formal structure of ownness: everything mine is mine by coinciding with the standing ego.

(351) My ego and everything my own, my modes of consciousness, my acts, my objects of consciousness: the mine has its mineness in this ego-centring through coincidence constantly actualizing itself in every presentification that occurs, and constantly maintaining itself, constantly retained through all sedimentation. I also say, actualization through the occurrence of original coincidence with the primally original standing ego in the primally original present, the constant source-ground. Mineness is ego-centring through coincidence, preserved through sedimentation. The living present is the "source-ground."

(351) The ego, the identical ego, has thus been shown in its ownness and not characterized by contrast with the others constituting themselves for me. The methodological point: ownness is defined positively, by coincidence of ego-poles, not negatively, by exclusion of others. This is a correction of the Fifth Meditation's method of defining ownness by abstraction from others.

(351) I, the reflecting ego, am human ego; my conscious life is world-conscious life. I myself am conscious of myself as human among fellow humans. The world, I myself, humans in general are conscious as world for all, I human for all, every object, every human among them, existing for all. Consciousness, here consciousness of validity, doxic consciousness, carrying certainty of being in itself, I as taking or having doxic position, which position, the thesis, can modalize. Constant world-consciousness: a universal connectedness of all doxothetic experiences that are mine, giving unity to all my explicit and implicit doxic theses. My conscious life is a universal unity through immanent temporalization. But a particular unity in it is world-certainty, changing in content in the change and yet finished in every living present. In the standing-streaming primally original present the streamingly temporalized stream of consciousness is constituted and constitutes itself, horizonally. I can follow this temporalized stream back and forward disclosing it. In it all my validities of being, certainties of being, modalizations, but also all fictions. World-certainty is a particular unity within the stream: "finished" in every present, though changing in content. The "standing-streaming" present is named. The heading that follows marks the turn to the habitual ego.

#### The Ego in Its Habitual Ownness

(351–352) A unity of a particular kind, constituted under the title world-certainty, holds through the "stream of consciousness" continuously as continuous unity of validity and of the valid, but so that single validities are constantly corrected, temporarily stuck in modalization. Identity of my ego, but now particular identity of the ego as living in certainty, holding certainty fast, uniting certainty with certainty, constituting a universe of what is valid to it, whose subject of validity it is. But certainties modalize; the ego in its certainty suffers a break; it can keep its remaining direction only temporarily. The ego changes its mode of being. As ego of validity it is the ego that is decided (entschieden), and is the same as long as it is so. It is universal world-ego; it is the same and persisting in its universal decidedness, which comprises the particular decidednesses. But it cannot hold them all; it cannot keep itself so purely in its universality of single positions. That gives difficult problems. Above all, once it is clarified what a single doxic certainty looks like and how it is lasting validity for the ego, in which it has being, one must understand the act of doxa and synthetic acts in which one doxic act and another, and so several in general, establish the unity of an act, and how an act-synthetic objectivity is constituted as "categorial." Husserl's note: decision is not a good word, since it already refers to modalization. The habitual ego is the "ego of decisions" (or decidedness): identical as long as it holds its positions. When certainties break, the ego changes its mode of being. The note concedes the term's defect, since "decision" presupposes prior doubt.

(352–353) But another thing is the connectedness that all acts have which my ego now performs as its decidednesses, as resumptions of decisions I made earlier and that still continue to be valid now, in which it is still now decided. Not all earlier acts, now recollected, belong to me, the ego that is decided, but only those remembered as earlier that manifest my decidedness from an earlier decision. Every new decision connects with the horizon of acts that explicate me as the ego I now am, the ego of decidedness. I do not change as this ego by taking up new decisions that are now my own as decidednesses. Rather, as this ego I am only the same in constant acquisition of new decidednesses; being an ego in this sense is being in deciding and persisting in one's decidednesses, and I am the same as long as in the progress of decisions I preserve my old decidednesses. So I am as preserving myself. But I am also, when I cannot maintain myself in single decisions, still the same insofar as in this non-maintenance, in the form of modalization, I overcome it by transforming the modalized earlier decision and decidedness into a new decidedness; the modalizations drop out, the old decidedness is crossed out, and in this transformation, in these modes of change of the ego as ego of decidednesses, the ego persists as the same, since in every moment of the primally living present it has come to a unity of self-preservation. That says: the whole life-stream of the ego identical in the wider sense, mine, is so constituted that in every primally original present it has the form of a unity of decidedness, or has it in foresight, striving toward it, in which indecisions, modalized decidednesses transform into decidednesses, establishing a totality of decidedness, and so the ego lives as ego of a universal certainty. But the whole matter has been considered only from one side, the past. The ego is constantly striving, in modes of striving, living as such directed forward in pre-intentions and in fulfilments or disappointments. All decidednesses are already modes of activity. Activity has its horizon of potential activity, of pre-validity. The ego's identity is self-preservation through decisions: it remains the same by preserving its decidednesses and, when one breaks, by converting the break into a new decidedness. The life-stream has in every present the form of a unity of decidedness or strives toward it. This is the theory of "self-preservation" (Selbsterhaltung) as the form of personal identity, close to Spinoza's conatus and to the ethical "self-responsibility" of the 1920s lectures.

(353) The constitution of the ego as constituting lasting certainty of being, as preserving itself in its totality of decidednesses and temporalizing itself, in the form of falling away from self-preservation and self-correction, as self-preserving, as ego persisting through its relative self-preservations: that is the constitution of the ego as person, as persisting subject for a universe of beings, for an existing universe, a world that is the correlate of the person, "person" in an exaggeratedly extended sense, but I find no word. Person is the self-preserving ego of decisions, correlate of a world of beings. "Person" here extends beyond the social sense, and Husserl notes the lack of a better word.

(353) But I, the reflecting one, am personal persisting subject of the world in the natural sense, the spatio-temporal world, the human world, in which I am human and the world is the same world for all humans, for all persisting human persons. The natural person is the same structure in the world.

(353) To me, to the person in general (this lies in the above), belongs my totality of beings, related to my whole conscious life and its continuous ego and to the unity of the persisting ego constituting itself and already constituted in it, in which a universe of decidedness has concentrated itself as self-preserving, in constant foresight of self-preservation and of what is to be effected practically from the ego. To every being belongs that it is the identical for which I am decided, unity of a manifold of decidednesses, settled as past, anticipated as future. A being is the identical to which I can come back again and again, which is always accessible to me again, immediately or mediately; accessible in originality insofar as I have "experienced" it, gained it from experience in certainty of being and now acquired it as decidedness, and so can actualize my decidedness again and again by going back to recollection in its consciousness of still-validity. Other certainty is anticipation in its way of what I have already acquired, pre-decided, pre-certainty. Being is the correlate of decidedness: "the identical to which I can always come back." The heading "Others" follows.

#### Others

(353) But how do others come in here? How does a being for me in my conscious life become a being for others, a being for all, and these themselves again beings and so beings for all, among which they themselves are included? The question of the intersubjective sense of being, posed against the egological theory of being as decidedness.

(354) Reflecting, going back to every beginning, I tell myself: everything that is for me is so as conscious to me, as valid to me with its content of sense in certainty of being, explicitly or implicitly, that is, accessible from what is properly conscious to me on paths of consciousness which, as I already know, I can freely perform. Reflecting, I soon notice that my perceptual real surroundings are a segment of what I actually perceive of "the" world; I can go further and further from there or think a going further in presentification of possible paths of experience. So I experience by actuality and possibility ever new worldly realities and types of reality, and in free variation of possibilities the structural typology of the world as world of possible experience. To it also belong fellow humans. If actually conscious, the same holds of them as of objects of consciousness in general; they are conscious straightforwardly or under ego-reflection, consciousness being self-giving (perceiving) or in various ways modification of perceiving. If the other is there for me himself, perceptually, I am, as I realize by reflection, with him himself; while I myself am in any case at the same time, as with everything conscious, primally originally, perceptually present. Others belong to the world as objects of possible experience, and when perceived, "I am with him himself," while also being present to myself primally.

(354) Every perception and experience of the real is "apperceiving." If I follow the apperceived as regards others when I perceive them, I find properly perceived and perceivable only their bodily corporeality. The other ego and all its conscious life, everything its own as ego, is and remains always only presentified; it can never be transformed for me into a presentation, a perception, though it is valid as a there-with, a co-present. The standard thesis restated: the other's ego is only presentified, never presentable.

(354) Empathic presentifications are distinguished in their essential peculiarity from all other presentifications. All the latter separate off as a closed universe (not a merely conceptual class), in which, when we subject them to reflection "in" the presentification and go back to the ego and ego-consciousness that is presentified correlate of the presentified objective, this ego and consciousness is evidently mine, that of the presentifier. Original reflection on the now existing presentification makes me and my presentifying, as now primally originally existing, thematic. Together with it, reflecting in the presentification on its ego and its perceiving and other being-conscious as presentified, the original identity at once emerges in the form: I, the ego of the primal present with the presentifying primally originally existing in it, am the same ego that was with the recollected in recollection, will be with the coming in expectation. The criterion: reflection "in" a presentification finds its ego. In memory and expectation it is I; in empathy it is not. Non-empathic presentifications form a "closed universe" by this criterion.

(354–355) All positional presentifications, all performed by me in the mode of validity of being, to which reflection leads me and can lead me, form among themselves and together with the continuously streaming perceptions a closed unity insofar as through reflection we find the perceiving ego and the reflected ego in the presentification identical in original evidence. All such presentifications I call my memories. What lies in the sense of this universe of original identification and its identical? I am the same ego that is now in actual primally original self-presence, am in streaming original life, and the same who was in my past life that was streaming present, the same who has before him his future as streamingly coming-to-be, coming to originally present life, the same ego in the universal horizon of being of its temporality, in its streamingly changing temporal modalities: my present, my past, my future. "Memories" in the widest sense: all presentifications whose ego coincides with mine. They constitute my temporality.

(355) From there I see: primally originally I am as ego of the streaming primally original present, to which already belongs a primally original change from primally welling originality into a no-longer-welling one, which as such, modifying originality, already creates the pre-form of presentification, that of retention, and then in the counter-direction protention. With this my first self-temporalization takes place, which, as is not to be explicated more closely here, as passive-primally-associative in the standing streaming, temporalizes the stream constituting itself there in its livingly extending temporality with its temporal modalities present (presentness of streaming), past (just-been streaming), future, phase-wise, stretch-wise and endlessly as stream, and all together as concrete subjective temporality without beginning, without end. The egoic affectivity and activity structurally contained in it yields identity of the actual ego-pole in this stream, and the capacity of reflection discloses the identical ego also in the anonymity of straightforward validities of consciousness passing through this stream continuously connected. In the primally original present or in the stream of consciousness temporally-modally extended, capably identifiable as temporally existing, with its identical ego (as primally original, perceptually existing for itself), "memories" capably occur, and belonging to them as to all acts the capacity of identifying repetition and of reflection. All presentification is itself something like "repetition," and reflection in the repetition, in the presentification, and in the repetition of the presentification itself is always possible. By means of presentifications a universal temporalization takes place, and, keeping exclusively to the presentifications in which subjective reflection leads to identity of the reflected ego (the ego in the presentification) with the primally original present ego, the totality of my, the identical ego's, temporal coexistence opens up or is constituted as existing; my all-temporality opens up as that of my being identical in the universality of my current present, past and future conscious life. In the self-temporalization taking place in the primal living present, my universal being as perceptual and memorial is constituted as universal immanent time (concretely in its filling); self-temporalization takes place ideally in the universe of my self-perceptions and self-memories, and this then functions further as universe of my own intentionality of consciousness, in which objective time is constituted secondarily. Self-temporalization in compressed form: retention as "pre-form of presentification," passive primal association, a stream "without beginning, without end." Presentification is repetition; reflection within it discloses my all-time. Objective time is constituted "secondarily" within this universe of self-perceptions and self-memories. The thesis that subjective time has no beginning and no end bears on the problems of birth and death (Beilage VIII).

(356) Now consider empathic presentifications. As my modes of consciousness, actively as my acts, they occur currently in my primally original present. But what they presentify leads, when I reflect in them, go back to the ego and consciousness presentified there, not to the same ego I myself am, only in another temporal modality, so not to a region of my life-temporality and my ego living (having once lived) in this region of time. Reflection in empathy finds an ego that is not mine in another temporal mode.

(356) But first the remarkable thing here is that the apperception-making actual and potential presentification belonging to every real and so to every other human, in the case of perception and its apperceived co-presence, cannot, and in principle cannot, be capably transformed into a perception, but this holds only of the bodiliness purely as corporeality, while what makes it a lived body and the body of a human person with human conscious life holds. The ego of this body, the other as other ego with his other conscious life, is in principle only presentified and can be brought to progressive clarity only in the manner of a presentification, likewise as to confirmation of its validity of being to progressive confirmation: so without the presentifications ever being transformable for me into perceptions. Nevertheless the concrete apperception of the human, not only as body but as this human, is perception. The paradox is restated with emphasis: the other's ego is only presentified, yet the concrete apperception of the human is "perception." Perception here includes an unredeemable appresentation as its essential component.

(356–357) If this holds of every perception through which another is there himself for me, then accordingly every memory of others, every mode of givenness of him presentifying in the manner of a memory in general, is presentifying at a higher, at least second level, namely as to the specifically psychic of the other ego and its intentional life. These memories, as modification of my perceptions, lead of course in inner reflection to myself, but in the second step, as to the presentified presentifications through which the other ego confronts me temporalized in a region of time outside the present of my immanent temporality, as moments of my presentified psychophysical perceptions, I come by inner reflection not to myself but to the other: to "I," but not I myself; the ego is alter ego. Memory of another is a second-level presentification: reflection in it finds first my past perceiving, then, within that, the other's presentified life. "I, but not I myself; the ego is alter ego": the formula states that the other is an "I" given within my reflection yet not identifiable with me, the exact point where egological reflection meets its limit.
